The owner confirmed the newly supplied physical-device CSVs are R375.

## Implementation/build handoff versus external acceptance

The current 3DS startup follow-up is the R376 experimental 3DSX. Physical
R180/R373/R375 failures supersede the earlier handoff-only assessment. R375's
trace locates the stall at the second-frame GPU queue wait. R376 corrects
partial shader outputs, while retaining the real pre-game menu, HUD routing,
New-model stereo / Original-model mono policy and deferred Preview-OFF PICA
initialization. Native compilation, all 47 host tests and strict Original
default Start/render/shutdown pass; resolution of the console freeze remains
unverified. The owner has no console locally. Do not repeatedly
request tester results while other relevant implementation work can proceed.

Current rendering build/package checks are complete for the Windows player,
R368 Apple packages and R374-signed R369 Android/R372 Quest handoffs. These
immutable packages are not rebuilt snapshots of later source edits. The
known PCVR gameplay process-exit investigation remains genuinely unresolved;
R370/R372 correct verified lifetime bugs but are not a reproduced crash fix.
Console FPS/audio/slider/sleep,
physical Apple/Android/Leia rendering and sustained headset stability remain
external acceptance, not evidence of new missing implementations. No release
or main update is implied by this handoff, and the full goal is not declared
complete merely from successful compilers and host checks.

Scope correction: the calibrated renderer and its 30-program Metal experiment
are not an unfinished normal-iPhone integration. The actual game selects
DisplayXR only in the Windows desktop build (`CMakeLists.txt`), and
`DisplayXrDesktop` chooses D3D12 or Vulkan. Normal Apple mono/stereo rendering
calls `MetalHardwareRt` directly. The exhaustive source-reflection validator
also requires explicit diagnostic opt-in; it is never a menu/config setting.
Its R358 deadline failure remains recorded below, but is not an ordinary-game
FPS result or evidence of an iPhone/3DS defect. Further work on that experiment
must be justified by a player-facing requirement, not by its existence.

previous independently SDK-compiled hashes. These are not GPU/runtime tests.

run38132420873, commit226e21f42dc272fe508d2d7cf6278c49806408b6. The immutable

The next target is fail-closed short-circuiting of an ALREADY INVALID optical
path. The scalar predicate's arithmetic-failure flag is monotone: only its
caller resets it per cell; all predicate callees can only set it true. Once
true, every remaining scalar return is necessarily false. Its native writer
observes that predicate only to keep/exclude the same original cell and hull;
it does not publish the intermediate arithmetic. Any experiment must prove
the exact boolean/failure result, retain every cell, original writer/coverage,
successful-path math and checks, and measure real failed-path work before
claiming a benefit. Jet output also exposes branch/failure diagnostics, so
scalar reasoning alone cannot authorize changing its early-exit behavior.
R335 now tests nine fail-closed scalar early exits, leaving all successful-path
math and the jet diagnostics unchanged. The first complete host transport
failed to map one HLSL `inout` qualifier; its failure logs are retained. The
separate corrected transport passes 24,104 complete predicate/refusal cases in
each gradual and FTZ/DAZ mode, with both scalar backend bodies. Unsafe acceptance
and overbroad-refusal controls are rejected. All successful-path work matches.
The finite-frame profile (without preset failure) reduces counted host arithmetic
from 5,371,426 to 5,267,880 in gradual mode and 5,372,426 to 5,308,700 in FTZ/DAZ:
only about 1–2%. This is not enough evidence to claim a GPU bottleneck fix;

October10 native Metal completion/cleanup follow-up: R303 adds nonblocking
completion polling and try-cleanup to the entire native optical producer,
plus output-image capacity accounting that excludes source/AS/scratch and
available VRAM. Submitted work without a fence retains every native allocation;
a later empty same-queue marker certifies completion without replaying or
cancelling the consumed command. Blocking cleanup certifies all three slots
before releasing any storage. A failed later wait cannot partially release
an earlier slot; failed completion retains the owner/device for explicit
retry and invalidates borrowed outputs. Failed destructor completion retains
GPU-visible storage rather than allowing ARC to free it. Ordinary slot reuse
retains its original wait behavior, now with missing-fence recovery.

October 7 R37 GPU necessary source-support follow-up (enclosure PASS,
production curved-colour reuse still NOT ACCEPTED):

The resident candidate index now feeds a second GPU pass that checks exact
old receiver/ordered-hop/terminal tags and clips per-tap feature-footprint
half-planes inside the retained tiles. Integer points, horizontal/vertical
zero-weight edges and 2D cells are separate supports. Valid matched neighbours
still contribute gradients when their noncontributing depth is invalid.
Each result contains its support, conservative bounds and a convex-polygon
centre initializer. This is a necessary source enclosure, not a physical
optical-root or uniqueness certificate. No incoming RGB, response/base words
or downloaded inverse selects a support.

Compensated binary32 arithmetic clips the polygons; outward query/serialization
enclosures and rounded old-eye direction upload do not alter the actual
source/optical/colour tolerances. A cheap outward anchor-feature cap eliminates
unrelated anchors before clipping. On every NVIDIA/Intel and D3D12/Vulkan
combination, the R34 two-branch input produces five GPU enclosures from 168
support attempts, versus 1,540 before the anchor cap. The R33 distant-source
input produces three from 86, versus 1,918. Independent binary64 domains are
enclosed support-for-support, not merely by a nearby tile. These are diagnostic
work counts, not a game frame-rate or whole-presentation cost measurement.

Independent binary64 forward/source-guard audits started from the actual GPU
initializers recover the same two visible R34 branches and one R33 branch as
the reference domain-centre starts. V3/V4 replays also retain their nine source
supports and recover the reference branch. This verifies those initializer
cases, not absence of other roots between starts; the two-branch case remains
explicitly ambiguous and forbidden for reuse. No GPU optical certification
or player colour reuse is enabled by this follow-up.

The GPU pass limits each query to 128 regions and 16,384 support attempts.
Any upstream leaf/node refusal, region/work overflow, invalid quota or
arithmetic/storage failure invalidates every emitted region. Host decoding
refuses partial results. The standalone upload/readback test limits batches
to 1,024 queries to bound its new region-buffer footprint; this is not a
player resolution or quality limit. Held queries neither upload/rebuild the
old bank nor alter the resident index; canonical source enclosures are
bit-exact across held/reordered queries and RGB/base/padding changes.

All eight actual replay/device/API checks pass, alongside odd/partial leaves,

invalid-depth gradient halos, all four support dimensions and 24 nonlinear

empty-lobe, capacity and teardown checks remain. D3D12 records zero critical
or discarded messages through owned-resource and SDL-device teardown, with
the same retained-native-COM limitation as R36. Seven focused host tests pass.

Initial builds rejected a reserved HLSL helper name and implicit C++ numeric

A diagnostic-only GPU hierarchy now indexes the complete accepted-old raw

The old bank is uploaded and indexed once in this standalone diagnostic.

October 6 native Vulkan lifetime follow-up (Windows pinned-owner checks,
NOT calibrated application integration, Linux/Deck, device-loss, FPS or final
3DS acceptance):

`native_work_complete()` queries completion without waiting/readback.
`try_release_device()` clears borrowed outputs but retains all resources until
every producer slot is complete. Explicit blocking cleanup and slot reuse
respect wait failures, rather than freeing GPU-visible storage on an error.
All slots are certified before any teardown. A consumed submission returning
no fence obtains an empty ordered recovery marker; it is not cancelled or
replayed. If completion cannot be established during destruction, the owner is
deliberately retained rather than freeing live resources. The borrowed SDL
device must outlive pending cleanup/retries; this is not a device-loss policy
accepted on a platform. External SDL consumers remain caller-owned.

Initialization and lazy reflection initialization now have explicit readiness
and rollback. Buffer allocation/bind failures destroy their local buffer/memory;
failed partial variant pipeline creation cannot satisfy a cached-ready check.
`working_image_bytes()` reports retained SDL shadow/reflection capacities,
including the larger full-water allocation after a smaller request, not VRAM,
source payloads, acceleration structures, scratch or driver allocations.

The dedicated diagnostic passes on NVIDIA RTX 5070 Ti Laptop and Intel Graphics.
Its linker wrappers affect only the checker, not players. It injects shadow/
reflection descriptor-pool initialization, buffer allocation/bind, partial
variant pipeline and post-dispatch recording failures. A real Vulkan timeline
gate keeps three producer slots queued and incomplete while checks verify no
poll/try-release wait, no native destruction and retained 86,016 image bytes.
Failed blocking waits/slot reuse and fence-query errors also retain resources.
After signalling, all native buffer/memory/AS/pipeline/descriptor handles retire.
Eight consumed-submit cases per GPU hide one/two real submitted fences over
both producers and repeated cycles. Recovery markers do not replay a native
dispatch or cancel consumed commands; valid retries exactly restore all 3,072
baseline pixels and release tracked handles/image capacities. No shader or
uniform/descriptor ABI change is introduced.

Full populated-scene owner regressions also pass on both GPUs: 1,648 shadow,
3,504 native reflection, 200 indexed reflection and 936 water submissions per
device, plus the resident sky, planar/lava and model-conductor suites. RGB and
ownership gates remain unchanged; malformed requests, slot reuse and release/
reinitialize recover correctly. The 672 empty reflection, 25 populated/empty
transition and 288 exact-clear shadow cases also pass on both GPUs, together
with ten malformed padded-payload/recovery checks per owner.

Fifteen rebuilt host tests pass, including unsupported Vulkan cleanup and
calibrated history/scene, raster/geometry, software reflection and temporal AA.
The generated Vulkan shader consistency check passes; Apple source assembly
is still a host check, not Metal compilation. Evidence:
`D:/SFE-validation/3ds-residency-oct4/native-lifetime-checkpoint-oct6.json`.
Early lifetime fixture builds/logs are not acceptance evidence: the bridge
lease was corrected to preserve SDL's cleanup-owned table and borrow a copy
of its separate read-only static ray table before final runs.

Reflection history/per-lobe/path outputs and calibrated application integration
remain open, together with Metal/platform acceptance, remaining effects and
current native 3DS packaging/device verification. Installed PC/PCVR players,
README and Git index are unchanged. No remote write or full-goal completion is
claimed. Earlier entries retain their own checkpoints.

October 6 native Vulkan empty-scene follow-up (Windows shader/owner checks,
NOT calibrated application integration, Linux/Deck, FPS or final 3DS acceptance):

The producer accepts the calibrated renderer's complete padded native payload
with zero source vertices. It builds a genuine zero-instance TLAS, without a
BLAS, manufactured triangle or source-vertex upload. Its reserved input address
is explicitly aligned to 16 bytes. Empty CPU shadow scenes also produce clear masks.
Both empty and populated builds query the device's required acceleration-structure
scratch alignment, overallocate appropriately and align the scratch address.
Incomplete or malformed native payloads remain rejected; indexed reflection
requests have not been broadened to an unsupported empty-material contract.

is exact. Normal error remains below 2e-5 and forward-depth error below

lava colour recipe still does not establish realism or stage appearance.

Twenty-five populated/empty submissions erase and exactly restore 1,517 model

Reflection/shadow uniforms remain 416/352 bytes. No shader change, extra source

October 6 native Vulkan model/conductor follow-up (Windows shader/owner checks,

Native linear/sRGB models now use the calibrated normal-offset primary bias,

secondary models with per-hit conductor attenuation and a four-bounce budget,
resolving the current resident sky at exit instead of a pre-mirror model image.
The sharp non-conductor fast path preserves packed transport. Legacy encoding0
retains its existing ray counts, palette recipe and tint behaviour.

The input-only model reference first failed the old shader on material-coloured
metal, linear format, fallback environment, pixel0 (4289755201/4280487940);

owner now pass on NVIDIA RTX 5070 Ti Laptop and Intel Graphics. Each runs 1,728
cases over four conductor modes, linear/sRGB, solid/RGBA/native-palette coverage,
three roughness values, global model transport ON/OFF, fallback/rotated cube,
inside-two-planes budget exits, grazing faces, asymmetric cameras and explicit

Three invalid specular encodings clear output and recover per owner, followed

Constant2 selects the cached native model pipeline; the cached water and planar/

October 6 Vulkan resident-environment follow-up (Windows hardware checks,
NOT Linux/Deck application or complete rendering/3DS acceptance):

Native reflections now accept `ResidentEnvironmentCube` metadata for the
calibrated renderer's existing GPU allocation. Six +X,-X,+Y,-Y,+Z,-Z faces
follow native material bytes; the eye-to-cube rotation is supplied explicitly.
The producer validates an 8..512 power-of-two face extent, contiguous/aligned
native payload, linear/sRGB encoding, orthonormal rotation and source range.
Materials and cube move together in one GPU-resident range copy. Source sky
pixels/vertices never visit the CPU in this production path. Native cube
requests skip unused indexed-palette and dummy-panorama/image allocations and
uploads; their unused descriptors reuse storage-capable vertices. Existing
cached enhanced artwork is retained. No extra descriptor or separate cube
allocation is added. Reflection uniforms are now 416 bytes; shadow uniforms
remain 352 bytes, superseding the reflection size in the entries below.

Cube taps cross edges by reconstructing/reprojecting their direction onto the
neighbour face, rather than stretching its last texel. sRGB taps decode before
linear-light interpolation and encode for native output. Cube alpha does not
make the sky/reflections transparent. Ordinary and native-water reflection
transport both use the rotated resident cube instead of the fallback colour.

October 5 audio compatibility progress: a separate asset-free CSND stereo check
has been added to the native frontend diagnostic (Y; NDSP remains X). It owns
immutable one-shot PCM on two actually granted channels, starts both sides in
one command batch and waits for actual channel status, not an estimated DMA
cursor. NDSP and CSND share a single process output lease. Acknowledgment polls
yield and have a 100 ms deadline; failed/unacknowledged lists retire and poison
the service owner before reuse. Service exit precedes linear-memory release.
The diagnostic does not import the reference's looping ring, frame insertion,
dropped packets or SPC worker scheduling. The game still uses NDSP and its
unchanged two accurate SPC stems. Native CSND streaming/clock matching and
Home/sleep integration are not implemented or accepted by this check.

clock wrap, channel shortages, repeat-after-completion and active-DMA protection.

October 5 native water/lava reflected-radiance owner: the development renderer
now connects the native liquid producer, accepted previous-wave metadata and
resident history consumer. Current and previous optics share the same
band-limited water normals, displaced lava hit/bubble gradients and exact
inverse-transpose world transform. Current transmission, molten emission,
direct light and material response remain separate from the filtered incoming
reflection. The authored lava recipe is decoded once when output is sRGB.
Lava rays escaping their displaced source surface no longer hit the flat
reference plane before the finite reflected actor.

The bounded old-wave solve is a separate compute dispatch after native RT,
in the same command list, submission and allocation. It consumes matched
native secondary witnesses; it adds no scene traversal, readback, submission,
fence wait or primary-TAA dependency. Ordinary reflection PSOs do not include
the inverse-wave solve. Failed optical paths stay invalid, not flat-plane
motion. The owner retains the actually accepted old plane, transform/offset,
eye projection and wave clock. Wave-only advancement renders again; rewind,
material/source/scene changes cut history; held/rejected presentations cannot
advance the accepted wave. Native lava is material 3 (authored ground choice
9), while red sand remains non-reflective. Working bounds include the full
liquid prefix and both history banks, not just RGBA.

October 4 independent reflected-radiance follow-up: sharp ordinary finite
single-bounce reflection history no longer depends on primary TAA. A separate

trace or six-face environment capture; shadows/fog still request live geometry.
Source-grid/setting/scene/flow/camera cuts and active-effect-clock rewinds reset
correspondence. The 52-byte ray layout and two history banks remain bounded;
the working limits are not a guarantee of available VRAM.

Fresh native owner checks pass on Windows D3D12 and Windows Vulkan/DXR interop,

reflected RGB bytes, proving the integration does not silently bypass the

per format, plus all 52 bytes of accepted/rejected/held resident history.
Five freshly rebuilt host suites, the strict resolver compile, and full

September 23 iOS physical startup: a sideloaded 0.0.6.7 package launched on
one iPhone after `Starfox-Assets.BIN` was supplied through iTunes File Sharing.
The published SDL file picker is unsupported on iOS; current source adds a
UIKit picker with main-thread dispatch, but it has not yet been built or tested

while a brief 1× run did not exit. No matching crash report was available, so
the 4× fault remains open. See GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md for the desktop GPU

September 23 GPU transition audit: Original and EX Game Over and Original

claimed. The ordinary Android arm64 Debug APK rebuilt and its package check

September 23 ordinary Android GPU startup mitigation: [issue #64](https://github.com/kandowontu/starfox-enhanced/issues/64)
reports a GPU-only freeze when entering preview or play; its reporter says
Software works. New/upgraded installs now select Software once, while GPU
remains an explicit option. A persistent GPU-in-progress marker recovers the
next launch to Software after a hang during renderer creation or the first
120 scene frames, re-arming on menu/scene transitions. Android `startup.log`
is now written beside `pregame.cfg` in app storage for device diagnosis.
The ordinary arm64 APK rebuild and package-content check pass. This is a
startup escape hatch, not a demonstrated Vulkan driver fix: no ordinary
Android handset is attached, and issue #64 remains open pending logcat and
physical retest. A GPU failure after the guarded transition window may still
require manually selecting Software.

Original Venom approach/escape cloud artwork is now mapped with its bank-5

Titania's weather changer is now exercised through normal flight input. The
native blue-fog to gold/brown transition is verified. Added a photographic
cloud backdrop using the live shade ramp, and fixed distant snow being treated

visual validation is not implied by the passing shader/reflection fixtures.

EX 5-1/6-1/7-1 acceptance includes the authored starting palettes and every
subsequent color transition; 7-1 has one fade, not a repeating enhancement
animation. Enhanced palette response is derived from live CGRAM and the upload
palette without an independent timer. Removed per-channel division that made
small red changes in 5-1's blue palette over-amplify into pink/orange. A uniform
brightness multiplier plus signed RGB offsets now maps each source palette
mean to its live mean, preserving uniform fades, black endpoints, and colors
introduced into initially zero channels. CPU, GPU and DXR reflection shading
use the same transform. Captured
5-1 and 7-1 palette values are covered by focused regression tests. Full
transition visual acceptance is recorded separately from CPU/GPU parity.
See [EX palette fade evidence](EX-PALETTE-FADES-SEPT20.md), including the 6-1
shared-artwork calibration and rejected intermediate renders.

User clarification: Titania's weather changer replaces the backdrop palette

backgrounds complete until this has been exercised and visually verified.

New queued requests: menu-only L+R held continuously for five seconds restores
default settings, with a countdown and release-to-rearm protection. Not implemented
yet. Investigate the reported GTX 750 Ti performance regression: GPU struggles and
Software drops into the 40s during heavy action; CPU, stage and exact settings are
unknown. Compare older/current builds under matching unenhanced settings and report

stall. The native-default benchmark now explicitly disables persisted environment,
material and manipulation settings; earlier runs may have inherited those options.

This is a navigation index, not a completion certificate. Feature documents
contain the actual runtime captures, commands, limitations and historical
checkpoints. Newer user instructions take precedence over the original goal:
VR/Steam physical validation is deferred, Software may keep Enhanced Shadows,
and the upgraded-laser label is Beam.

Latest EX background progress: all 38 menu choices now have independent Snes9x
reference captures using unmodified EX/controller navigation only, plus read-only
PPU register records. These supersede the guessed Mode 2 atlas-origin overrides:
host entry retained the wrong BG3 offset row and disabled its table. Correcting
both restores the nebula in choice 2 and Macbeth framing; Mode 1 retains its
individual source X/Y offsets. Menu BG2 is separated from UI. Ten menu choices
have photographic replacements, including city and orbital assets; EX orbital
entry scenes also have a replacement surface with the unique orange moon kept.
Full visual alignment and missing artwork remain open. Scramble atlases no longer inherit
checkerboard tunnel scanlines; source transition checks cover 5-1/6-1/7-1.
Remaining artwork and full visual acceptance are still open. See
EX-BACKGROUND-COVERAGE.md for exact evidence and limits.

September 20 latest environment batch: fixed integer division flattening the
enhanced horizon at small banks; shared CPU/GPU/DXR parameters now retain

Auto ground uses the same material response as explicit selection; matching
banked Corneria Auto/Grass final captures are byte-identical. Mirror fallback
uses exact plane mapping with protected bilinear taps; photographic DXR
reflections retain subpixel coordinates. Windows D3D12 and Linux Lavapipe
effects checks pass; hardware DXR checks include seven distinct colour steps
within one native-pixel camera movement and time-invariant mirror receivers.
Evidence and limitations: ENVIRONMENT-OPTIONS.md. No headset/release update.

Latest terrain follow-up: GPU terrain now supplies the lighting/reflection
normals required by active effects. Full Corneria 4x grass/dirt/sand/snow
native and presentation captures match software exactly; generated terrain

sustained-performance acceptance. Details: GPU-MIGRATION-STATUS.md.

September 20 follow-up: photographic Aurora now honors Sky Motion OFF in

pass, as does the hardware DXR checker. This is targeted environment validation,
not proof of every backdrop or completion of the migration.

## Additional user-requested work still open

- Enhanced Sky's current shared library contains 38 artwork resources,
  including the face moons, separate orbital surfaces and Kazaru's low
  volcanic hills. Current landscape/menu assignments, dedicated celestial
  atlases and abstract radial treatments supersede the historical blanket
  statement that those implementations are missing. This source inventory
  is not full visual acceptance: unsampled routes, transitions and physical
  presentation still need review. See `enhanced_backdrop_library.hpp`,
  `environment_effects.hpp`, `EX-BACKGROUND-COVERAGE.md` and the dedicated
  celestial/background coverage documents for their actual scopes.
- Enhanced terrain has geometry and targeted captures, not all-stage visual or
  sustained-performance acceptance. Ray secondary-hit fidelity and the horizon
  blend still need broader review.
- Both SDL effects and DXR now cache unchanged backdrop image uploads;
  sustained all-scene performance still requires wider measurement.
- Hardware-dependent acceptance must not be inferred from a successful build,
  a mock device, a package inspection or a software reference comparison.
- The October 12 consolidated New-3DS package is currently with testers. Its
  ARM closure matches the current 277 compiled inputs, and all 89 fresh
  New-3DS host cases pass. Those are source/game correctness checks, not a
  Windows rendering-suite pass or sustained physical New-3DS FPS acceptance.
  Keep that exact package unchanged while the consolidated playtest runs.
- The complete Windows moving-reflection owner is still unaccepted: R458
  fails its original completion deadline. R465's paired endpoint work-sharing
  correction is in source-only CI run 38290547809, with the original complete
  R466 GPU check queued afterward. No implementation, case, work bound,
  deadline or precision requirement is waived by this status correction.

Keep the full goal active until its actual requirements are proven. Do not
close open GitHub issues, publish a release, deploy to a headset or discard
user data merely to make this index look complete.

October 3 compatibility addition: desktop Windows x64 now has an optional
direct SR Platform output alongside DisplayXR. See [SR-PLATFORM.md](SR-PLATFORM.md)
for setup, exact local artifacts and scoped checks. Missing-runtime fallback,
owned BGRA texture handling and Preview-OFF behavior pass locally; physical SR
panel/lens acceptance and broader native enhancement parity remain open.
This addition does not complete the full goal or update the published release.

October 3 native liquid/TAA follow-up: reconstruction-first paths now defer
packet palette styles in ray materials and environment faces as well as the
primary raster. This prevents optical liquid radiance from receiving a style
before reconstruction and again in the ordered centre-grid post pass. The
independent full-image fixture now draws the backdrop before actors, matching
game assembly; its required wet/dry/protected coverage is retained, not waived.
D3D12 and Vulkan each pass 108 cases over four colour formats and three TAA
qualities, including banked/moving water and eyes, held frames, rejected-frame
recovery and nonblocking cancellation. Evidence:
`D:/SFE-validation/taa-liquid-post-raw-rays-actors-oct3/results.json`.
This is post-order/ownership validation against independent unstyled timelines,
not an independent optical water/depth-finish oracle, true secondary motion,
physical Leia-panel acceptance or a performance claim. The full goal stays open.

October 9 checkpoint: the authorized source-only shader CI completes all six
jobs. Independent artifact checks retain all requested shader inputs and
precision; the Vulkan candidate changes only the optical polynomial loop
control hint, not its arithmetic. The complete Vulkan runtime check stops at
the original memory floor during pipeline creation, before numerical cases.
It is not a Vulkan runtime pass. The unchanged full moving-reflection-owner
diagnostic then records D3D12 `0x887a0006` (device hung), without a resource
stop. Sharp reflection phases complete, but the rough eight-lobe moving phase
does not. The responsible command/shader and full rendering acceptance remain
open; no Windows timeout or test deadline is extended.

The current-source 3DS joined-audio candidate independently passes its actual
ARM SDK build, asset-free package/ELF/stack checks and CI host tests. An
experimental tester ZIP and a short matching installation guide are available
in `build/StarFoxEnhanced-3ds-test-r180*`. It is not physical-console/FPS
acceptance. An observer-only sharing-error repair preserves all 42 ordinary
native input edges and shutdown/state-restoration gates. That actual current
package passes the full emulator route, independently including pregame and
gameplay logic/audio activity, clean guest/host exit and state restoration.
The complete 50-case host suite resource-stopped after 23 passes; the remaining
27 cases are unaccepted. Earlier resource stops and prelaunch observer
failures remain failures, with their logs preserved. Production
integration and physical New-3DS stereo/original-3DS mono, audio, Home/sleep,
campaign and sustained performance checks are still required. No release is
published by these temporary test branches, and the full goal remains active.
